L1443: ## **Reviewer's comment:**
L1444: *The Applicant's E-R analyses for efficacy were based on the exposure metrics derived from the original popPK model. Because the parameter estimates by original model are very similar to the updated ones, the update of the popPK model is not expected to significantly impact the derived exposure metrics for each individual patient from the phase 3 trial EFC14028. Thus, the E-R analyses for efficacy based on the exposure metrics derived from the original popPK model is still considered acceptable. However, it is not appropriate to fix the intercept at 0. Further, given that this is univariate analysis, potential imbalance in patient's baseline factors across the exposure quantiles could have contributed to the negative trend of the E-R relationship.*
L1445: *Upon request, the Applicant summarized and compared the baseline demographic and disease characteristics by different exposure quartiles (Table 85). Consistent with the popPK findings on body weight effect, heavier and older patients were mostly distributed in higher AUC quartiles 3 and 4. In addition, patients in the lowest AUC quartile 1 appear to have higher FVC and 6MWT at baseline compared to quartile 4, which may have contributed to the negative E-R relationship for efficacy.*

L1450: ## 14.5.2.2. Exposure-Response Analyses for Safety
L1451: Upon the FDA's request, the Applicant conducted multivariate E-R analyses for safety using the pooled data from trials TDR12857, LTS13769, EFC14028, and ACT14132. The evaluated safety endpoints included any TEAEs, treatment-emergent SAEs, severe TEAEs, protocol-defined infusion associated reaction (IAR), algorithm-defined IARs, nasopharyngitis, headache, diarrhea, back pain, fall, and nausea. Comprehensive blood sampling schedules were implemented in all clinical trials to allow for noncompartmental analysis (NCA). Thus, the multivariate E-R analyses for safety were conducted based on the AUClast determined by NCA. Both AUClast and log(AUClast) were evaluated. Analyses were performed in all patients and each subgroup of patients with IOPD, patients with LOPD, treatment-naïve and non-naïve patients. For safety endpoints included in the Applicant's exposure-response analyses, no significant trend of higher incidence of events for higher exposure is observed. Table 86 shows the multivariate logistic regression results for the probability of experiencing a TEAE.
L1452: **Table 86. Logistic Regression Representing the Probability of Experiencing a TEAE as Function of AUC or log(AUC) Adjusted by Baseline Covariates (Age at Baseline, Disease Type/Treatment-Naïve/Treatment Experienced)**

L1456: ## **Reviewer's comment:**
L1457: *The Applicant's E-R analysis for safety is acceptable. No major positive exposure-safety relationship was identified in patients with LOPD and IOPD at the studied dose and exposure range. The safety profiles from the pediatric patients with IOPD are considered adequate to support safety in the extrapolation based on exposure matching strategy for pediatric patients with LOPD (discussed in the following section).*
L1458: ## **14.5.3. Pediatric Dose Selection**
L1459: Although the enrollment for the LOPD phase 3 trial (EFC14028) was open to patients 3 years and above, only one pediatric patient with the age of 16 years was included in the analyses. To determine whether the dosing regimen of 20 mg/kg qow is appropriate across different age or body weight groups, the Applicant conducted simulation to evaluate the exposure following 20 mg/kg for patients with LOPD who were 1 year of age and older upon FDA's request. The virtual population for pediatric age groups (&lt;18 years) was generated based on CDC weight-for-age growth chart. The virtual population for adults (≥18 years) was consistent with body weight from the available adult patients from trials TDR12857 and EFC14028 (n=74). The body weight distribution in virtual patients by age group is shown in Figure 36.
L1460: **Figure 36. Body Weight in Virtual Patients by Age Group**
L1461: Source: Applicant's response to an IR "PK response-12mar2021.pdf"
L1462: Note: Box plot: 5th, 25th, median, 75th, and 95th percentiles, Red symbols: mean; Black symbols: individual values (minimum to 5th percentile and 95th percentile to maximum); Blue: 1 to &lt;2 years; Orange: 2 to &lt;6 years; Pink: 6 to &lt;12 years; Cyan: 12 to &lt;18 years; Green: &gt;=18 years
L1463: Abbreviation: WT, weight
L1464: Based on the Applicant's simulation, when received the dose of 20 mg/kg qow, smaller patients tend to have lower exposure. Compared to the median exposures in adult patients, patients within 1 to &lt;2 years of age are expected to have approximately 60% lower exposures; patients within 2 to &lt;6 years of age are expected to have approximately 50% lower exposure; patients within 6 to 12 years of age are expected to have approximately 30% lower exposure; and patients within 12 to 18 years of age are expected to have approximately 12% lower exposure (Figure 37). These results indicated that smaller pediatric patients would need higher dose to achieve a comparable exposure to adult patients.
L1465: **Figure 37. Simulated Avalglucosidase Alfa AUC_{0-2w} and C_{max} by Age Category (1 to &lt;2 Years, 2 to &lt;6 Years, 6 to &lt;12 Years, 12 to &lt;18 Years, and \ge 18 Years Old) at 20 mg/kg Every Other Week in Patients With LOPD**
L1466: Source: Applicant's response to an IR "PK response-12mar2021.pdf"
L1467: Note: Box plot: 5th, 25th, median, 75th, and 95th percentiles, Red symbols: mean; Black symbols: individual values (minimum to 5th percentile and 95th percentile to maximum)
L1468: Pediatric patients with LOPD are expected to have similar response to the ERT treatment compared to the adult patients with LOPD (refer to Section 6.3.2). Thus, PK extrapolation approach based on modeling and simulation was utilized to select the appropriate dosing regimen for pediatric patients with LOPD. FDA conducted independent simulation to compare the exposures at 40 mg/kg and 20 mg/kg across different body weight groups. As shown in Figure 13, for subjects with body weight &lt;30 kg, 40 mg/kg qow is expected to provide a similar or slightly higher exposure (AUC_{2w}) compared to that in adult patients receiving 20 mg/kg qow (target exposure, blue dash line), while within the observed range from the patients with IOPD received 40 mg/kg in trial ACT14132. Similarly, for subjects weighing 30 kg and above, the exposure at 20 mg/kg qow is predicted to be within the observed range in adult patients with LOPD from trial EFC14028. In addition, no apparent E-R relationship was identified for safety endpoints in patients with IOPD and LOPD following the dosing regimen of 20 or 40 mg/kg qow. During the review, 40 kg was evaluated as an alternative body weight cut-off considering the following:
L1469: - 40 kg is closer to the lower end of the observed body weight range of patients receiving 20 mg/kg (38 kg-129 kg) in the LOPD trial EFC14028. There is very limited efficacy data available at exposure level of 20 mg/kg in patients weighing 30-40 kg.
- For subjects weighing 30 to &lt; 40 kg, a considerable proportion (&gt;30%) of patients who receive 20 mg/kg would have AUC2W lower than the 5th percentile of simulated AUC2W in adults receiving 20 mg/kg.
- The predicted AUC2W for patients who receive 40 mg/kg in this body weight range would be higher and still within the range observed in patients with IOPD receiving 40 mg/kg.
L1470: However, there is limited safety data at the higher dose/exposure level in patients with IOPD to support 40 mg/kg in patients weighing 30-40 kg. Thus, FDA recommended 40 mg/kg for pediatric patients weighing &lt;30 kg, and 20 mg/kg for patients weighing \ge30 kg.
L1471: ## **Applicant's Position Based on Alglucosidase Alfa (Lumizyme®)**
L1472: To support the newly proposed dosing regimen for patients with LOPD with the age of 1 year old and above, the Applicant provided following justifications:
L1473: *[Redacted text]*
L1474: ## **Reviewer's comment:**
L1475: *Based on the label for alglucosidase alfa (Lumizyme®), the youngest alglucosidase alfa-treated patient in the LOPD trial was 16 years of age. Thus, there is lack of efficacy data in younger patients with lower body weight, and the Applicant did not provide any data for alglucosidase alfa from younger patients during the review cycle for avalglucosidase alfa. In addition, "early-onset" is likely associated younger age and lower body weight. The Applicant's statement in the second bullet indicated that 40 mg/kg may provide additional benefit for patients with*
L1476: *younger age and lower body weight compared to 20 mg/kg qow. However, it is difficult to define the “severity” of the disease course in the label. (b) (4) based on the experience with alglucosidase alfa, 40 mg/kg would be a better option for younger patients with lower body weight.*
L1477: ## Applicant's Position Based on Avalglucosidase Alfa
L1478: - Data with avalglucosidase alfa do not support a lower efficacy in Pompe patients with lower body weight. No evidence of a PK/PD or PK/efficacy relationship was observed at 20 mg/kg qow in patient with LOPD in trial EFC14028.
- The lower plasma exposures in patients with lower body weight are correlated with a higher clearance, the likely result of faster cellular uptake in patients with lower body weight. Avalglucosidase alfa is cleared from the plasma by cellular uptake and then internalized into the lysosomes.
L1479: ## *Reviewer's comment:*
L1480: - *The observed PK/PD or PK-efficacy relationship for efficacy in trial EFC14028 was based on single dose level of 20 mg/kg, with the lowest body weight included in the analysis being 38 kg associated with a 16 years old subject. No E-R information is available at dose/exposure lower than the observed range, or in patients with younger age and lower body weight to rule out potential lower efficacy in this group.*
- *There is no data/evidence provided to support the hypothesis of faster cellular uptake in patients with lower body weight. Even if it is true, higher cellular uptake may only contribute partially to the lower plasma exposure of avalglucosidase alfa. The effect of body weight on lower plasma exposure could not be excluded. (b) (4)*
L1481: *The safety of 40 mg/kg qow in patients with body weight &lt;30 kg is supported by the safety data from patients with IOPD (weighing 9.9 to 63.5 kg) following 40 mg/kg qow regimen, in which the observed avalglucosidase alfa concentration could cover the model predicted exposure following 40 mg/kg qow in patients with LOPD whose body weight is &lt;30 kg. More importantly, sufficient early intervention is critical for the improved prognosis in patients with LOPD with relatively earlier onset. Therefore, 40 mg/kg qow for younger patients with body weight less than 30 kg is likely to provide a more favorable benefit-risk profile compared to 20 mg/kg qow.*
L1482: In conclusion, FDA recommends the following dosing regimen for patients ages 1 year and above with LOPD:
L1483: - For patients with body weight &lt;30 kg, 40 mg/kg qow
- For patients with body weight 30 kg and above, 20 mg/kg qow

L1486: ## 16. Efficacy: Additional Information and Assessment
L1487: ## Applicant's Tipping Point Analysis for Primary Efficacy Endpoint in EFC14028 (COMET)
L1488: The Applicant performed a tipping point analysis to investigate the severity of departures from the missing-at-random (MAR) assumption needed to overturn the noninferiority (NI) conclusion from the primary analysis in trial EFC14028. The tipping point analysis proceeded as follows:
L1489: - Step 1: Missing values for the primary endpoint (change from baseline to week 49 in FVC (% predicted)) were explicitly imputed based on the MAR assumption using multiple imputation methods. Specifically, missing data were imputed 1000 times to generate 1000 complete datasets with the SAS PROC MI procedure. A two-step approach was used as follows:Intermittent (nonmonotone) missing data were imputed first using a Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) method within each treatment arm
- The remaining monotone missing data were imputed using the sequential regression method, where a separate regression model was estimated for imputation of FVC (% predicted) at each time point. Each regression model included treatment, baseline FVC (% predicted), age, gender, FVC (% predicted) values at all previous visits.
- Step 2: The imputed values in step 1 were shifted in favor of alglucosidase alfa. In particular, the imputed values in the avalglucosidase alfa arm were decreased by \delta_1 (&gt;0) while the imputed values in the alglucosidase alfa arm were increased by \delta_2 (&gt;0). Note that a higher value of the primary endpoint was indicative of a better improvement in FVC (% predicted).
L1490: Table 87 presents the results of the tipping point analysis.

L1539: ## FVC (% Predicted) Over Time for Patients Not Having Measurements at Week 49 in EFC14028 (COMET)

Figure 45 presents the time profile of the FVC (% predicted) for the eight patients. The following are the key observations:
L1541: - Patients [REDACTED] (b) (6) and [REDACTED] (b) (6) did not discontinue the trial prior to week 49. Their FVC (% predicted) values after week 49 are available. The two patients in the avalglucosidase alfa arm showed numerical improvement at week 25 compared to baseline and then numerical decline from week 25 to week 37. FVC (% predicted) value of patient [REDACTED] (b) (6) appears to be recovered at week 73. It is difficult to statically judge whether the MAR assumption is appropriate for the three patients.
- Five patients in the alglucosidase alfa arm discontinued the trial prior to week 49. Two of them did not have any postbaseline values; two of them showed numerical improvement at their last visits compared to baseline; one of them showed slight decline over them. In general, discontinuation of these five patients does not appear to be related to lack of efficacy.

L1580: ## 19. Other Drug Development Considerations: Additional Information and Assessment
L1581: ## Active Comparator: U.S.-Lumizyme and EU-Myozyme Information
L1582: The review team sent an information request to clarify whether the drug product batches used as the active comparator, alglucosidase alfa, in the clinical trials was the U.S.-licensed Lumizyme. The Applicant responded that the clinical trial drug product batches included both U.S.-licensed Lumizyme and EU-approved Myozyme, and that the U.S.-licensed Lumizyme is the same product as the EU-approved Myozyme. The manufacturing scale for the drug product has changed over time (b) (4). Alglucosidase alfa produced at the (b) (4) scale was approved in the EU in February 2009 for Myozyme and approved in the United States in May 2010 as Lumizyme under BLA 125291.
L1583: The Applicant provided the release results for U.S.-licensed (Lumizyme) and EU-approved (Myozyme) alglucosidase alfa drug substance, formulated drug substance, and drug product batches that were used in the clinical trials EFC14028 and ACT14132. For clinical and commercial manufacturing of alglucosidase alfa, only the (b) (4) scale is currently used. All drug product batches for the clinical trials were manufactured after approval of the (b) (4) scale (Table 91). The alglucosidase alfa drug product manufacturing process, formulation, and container closure systems are the same for the U.S.-licensed Lumizyme and EU-approved Myozyme.

L1586: Source: Response to FDA Clinical Information Request dated January 28, 2021
L1587: Two representative executed batch records for alglucosidase alfa drug substance were also provided, one for a Myozyme batch that was used in the comparator arm of the avalglucosidase alfa clinical trial and one for a recently manufactured Lumizyme batch.
L1588: Based on this information, we conclude that the release results from the one Lumizyme batch and nine Myozyme batches that were used in the clinical trials as well as the executed batch records for Lumizyme and Myozyme support the claim that Lumizyme and Myozyme are the same product. Therefore, these two products are adequate to be used as the active comparator for the clinical trials, and use of both in the clinical trials would not have any adverse impact upon safety or efficacy.
L1589: ## 20. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L1590: See Section 7.7.6 and the clinical inspection summary dated March 19, 2021 (Marks et al. 2021) for details of the inspection.
L1591: ## 20.1. Biopharmaceutical Inspection
L1592: ## Issue
L1593: Delay in timing of biopharmaceutical inspections due to travel restrictions to the COVID19 pandemic.
L1594: ## Background
L1595: The primary analytical site generated the 11 ADA assay validation reports used to support the phase 3 pivotal trial, EFC14028. The assays were conducted at the Sanofi/Genzyme Biomarker and Clinical Bioanalysis (BCB) Boston facility in Framingham, MA. This facility was last inspected by OSIS in 2004, which was prior to the facility being acquired by Sanofi United States and Genzyme.
L1596: ## Assessment
L1597: Due to travel restrictions, the review team considered the possibility of a remote record review (RRR), but later determined that an RRR would not be able to be completed within the PDUFA timeline due to current workloads and the public health emergency. The review team determined that the various immunogenicity assays used in the pivotal clinical trials were suitable for the intended purpose. In addition, the available clinical immunogenicity data confirm acceptable in-study assay performance, and the BCB facility is an experienced bioanalytical facility involved in clinical pharmacology and immunogenicity studies for multiple Genzyme ERT BLAs, including BLA 125141 for Lumizyme and BLA125291 for Myozyme. Therefore, the lack of an OSIS bioanalytical inspection during the current review cycle is not considered a potential approvability issue.
L1598: ## Conclusion
L1599: The review team concluded that waiver of the biopharmaceutical inspection is acceptable. In addition, no postmarketing studies are necessary.

8 USE IN SPECIFIC POPULATIONS	In Section 8.1, updated the risk summary to reflect that there is not enough information about the risks of avalglucosidase alfa in pregnant women. Updated the embryo-fetal toxicity data. Updated reporting of exposure during pregnancy to "Pregnant women exposed to NEXVIAZYME, or their healthcare providers, should report NEXVIAZYME exposure by calling 1-800-745-4447, extension 15500."
In Section 8.2, added the sentence "Available published literature suggests the presence of alglucosidase alfa (another hydrolytic lysosomal glycogen-specific enzyme) in human milk."
In Section 8.4, indicated that the safety and effectiveness of avalglucosidase alfa have only been established in pediatric patients 1 year of age and older and not in pediatric patients younger than 1 year of age.
In Section 8.5, indicated that clinical trials of avalglucosidase alfa did not include sufficient numbers of patients 65 years of age and older to determine whether they respond differently from younger adult patients.

L1607: ## 22. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L1608: The following PMR and PMC were agreed upon with the Applicant and will be issued at the time of approval.
L1609: ## PMR
L1610: - PMR 4026-1Conduct a worldwide, descriptive safety study that collects data in women and their offspring who are exposed to Nexviazyme (avalglucosidase alfa-ngpt) during pregnancy and/or lactation to assess risk of pregnancy and maternal complications, adverse effects on the developing fetus and neonate, and adverse effects on the infant. Outcomes of exposed infants, including growth and development, will be assessed through at least the first year of life. The study will collect information for 10 years.
- PMR Schedule Milestones:Draft Protocol Submission: 02/2022
- Final Protocol Submission: 10/2022
- Study Completion: 10/2032
- Final Report Submission: 04/2033

L1696: **Drug:** neoGAA; a recombinant human acid \alpha-glucosidase (rhGAA) expressed in Chinese Hamster Ovary (CHO) cells, conjugated to multiple copies of a synthetic bis-mannose-6-phosphate-containing hexasaccharide (Genz-669342).
L1697: ## **Molecular formula/molecular weight:**
L1698: alglucosidase alfa moiety: C_{4488}H_{6826}N_{1196}O_{1297}S_{33} (approximate molecular formula)
L1699: Genz-669342 moieties (6-8 glycan units conjugated): C_{42}H_{75}N_3O_{40}P_2
L1700: NeoGAA has an approximate total molecular weight of 120,000 Da.
L1701: **Pharmacological class:** enzyme replacement therapy
L1702: **Indication:** Pompe disease
L1703: **Route of administration:** intravenous
L1704: ## **Background:**
L1705: The Sponsor submitted this IND for the development of neoGAA (recombinant human \alpha-glucosidase conjugated with synthetic bis-mannose-6-phosphate-Man6 glycan) for treatment of Pompe disease. NeoGAA is produced by conjugating a synthetic glycan (Genz-669342) to oxidized sialic acid residues on rhGAA through a synthetic linker with an aminoxyl moiety. The synthetic linker portion of Genz-669342 is structurally similar to hydroxylamine, which has the potential to be genotoxic. However, Genz-669342 was negative in the Ames test and chromosomal aberration assay. In the initial IND submitted on 03/28/2013, the Sponsor proposed a specification limit of (b)(4) \mug/mL for unconjugated Genz-669342 in the drug product. At this limit, the maximum potential dose of unconjugated Genz-669342 from neoGAA would have been (b)(4) mg/infusion (assuming a 60-kg body weight, and a maximum dose of 20 mg/kg neoGAA in the proposed phase 1 clinical study), which greatly exceeded the recommended ICH qualification threshold (b)(4) mg/infusion for a 60-kg patient). Although the neoGAA batch used in the toxicology studies contained unconjugated Genz-669342, the pivotal 26-week IV toxicity study in monkeys did not provide an assurance of safety for the proposed specification limit of (b)(4) \mug/mL. Therefore, on April 25, 2013, the Sponsor agreed with the Agency's recommended specification limit of (b)(4) \mug/mL unconjugated Genz-669342 (free glycan) in the proposed Phase 1 clinical trial. Subsequently, the Sponsor conducted a 3-month IV toxicity study in monkeys with neoGAA spiked with free glycan (unconjugated Genz-669342), to support the safety of a higher limit in future clinical studies.
L1706: In the current amendment, the Sponsor submitted the 3-month IV toxicity study in monkeys using neoGAA spiked with free glycan. The study is reviewed below.

L1714: ## Key Study Findings
L1715: Cynomolgus monkeys were treated with 50 mg/kg neoGAA spiked with 3, 6, or 12.55 mg/kg glycan (unconjugated) every other week intravenously. Emesis and vomitus were observed in all treatment group animals without apparent dose-dependent effects following infusion. The test-article at all doses produced slight to moderate reductions (not significant) in absolute and relative weights of male reproductive organs (epididymis, prostate and testis). No histopathological changes were associated with reduced male reproductive organ weights. The NOAEL for glycan was considered to be 12.55 mg/kg.
L1716: ## Methods
L1717: Doses: neoGAA (GZ402666): 50 mg/kg
glycan (b) (4) unconjugated Genz-669342): 3,
6, or 12.55 mg/kg
Frequency of dosing: Bi-weekly (once every two weeks)
Route of administration: intravenous infusion (for approximately 6 hours/day) via a catheter implanted in a jugular vein
Dose volume: 20 mL/kg/dose, 3.33 mL/kg/hr
Formulation/Vehicle: 10 mM histidine, pH 6.2, 0.01% Tween® 80, 2% mannitol, and 2% glycine
Species/Strain: cynomolgus monkeys
Number/Sex/Group: 4
Age: 2 to 3 years old
Weight: 2.2 to 2.9 kg for males and 2.2 to 2.9 kg for females
Satellite groups: 1/sex/group
Unique study design: Spiking of glycan at different concentrations in neo-GAA
Deviation from study protocol: The impact of deviation on interpretation of results is discussed in the Dosing Solution Analysis section.

L1721: ## Observations and Results
L1722: ## Mortality
L1723: Mortality was observed daily. There were no deaths.
L1724: ## Clinical Signs
L1725: Cageside observations were conducted for each animal once daily, and detailed observations were conducted weekly.
L1726: One male in the 50 mg/kg neoGAA + 6 mg/kg glycan group had vomitus and/or emesis on days 29 and 43 (doses 3 and 4) and erythema and swelling in the face on day 29 (dose 3), which occurred at approximately 1 hour after infusion and started diminishing approximately 15 minutes later.
L1727: As shown in the Sponsor's table below, vomitus was observed within 30 minutes after infusion in two males and three females in the 50 mg/kg neoGAA + 3 mg/kg glycan group, four males and one female in the 50 mg/kg neoGAA + 6 mg/kg glycan group, and two females in the 50 mg/kg neoGAA + 12.55 mg/kg glycan on dosing days 29, 43, 57, 71, and/or 85.
L1728: The Sponsor concluded that these observations were due to an infusion-related hypersensitivity reaction, and were not considered adverse because they were transient, not dose-limiting, resolved without veterinary intervention, and did not affect body weight. However, the same or similar reactions were not observed in monkeys treated with 50 mg/kg neoGAA bi-weekly in the 26-week intravenous infusion toxicity study (# 0658-11097) that was submitted in the initial IND. It is noted that these two studies were conducted at different test facilities. Thus, it is likely that the vomitus was due the presence of glycan in the test article, although this effect did not appear to be dose-dependent.

L1733: ## Body Weights
L1734: Body weights were recorded during the pre-dose phase, on day -1, and weekly thereafter during the dosing phase. There were no treatment-related changes in body weight or body weight gain.
L1735: ## Feed Consumption
L1736: Qualitative food consumption was recorded once daily. No test article-related effect on qualitative food consumption was observed.
L1737: ## Ophthalmoscopy
L1738: Ophthalmic examinations were conducted once during the pre-dose phase and once on day 88 of the dosing phase. There were no treatment-related changes.
L1739: ## ECG and Vital Signs
L1740: Body temperature, respiration, and heart rates were collected on animals anesthetized with ketamine once during the pre-dose phase and once on days 2, 30, and 86 of the dosing phase (approximately 24 hours after the end of infusion). Body temperature measurements were also collected on day 25 of the recovery phase. Respiration and heart rates were not monitored during the recovery phase because of the absence of abnormalities at the end of the dosing phase.
L1741: Blood pressure measurements were recorded in animals anesthetized with ketamine once during the pre-dose phase and on days 2, 30, and 86 of the dosing phase (approximately 24 hours after the end of infusion). Blood pressure was not monitored during the recovery phase because of the absence of abnormalities at the end of the dosing phase.
L1742: There were no adverse effects on heart rate or blood pressure (systolic, diastolic and mean arterial pressures).
L1743: Reduced respiratory rate (18% in mid-dose males, 22% in high-dose males, and 14% in high-dose females) was observed on day 30 only. Thus, reduced respiratory rate was not considered to be treatment-related.
L1744: Males in all treatment groups had a slight increase in body temperature, ranging from 0.2 to 1.1°C, during the treatment period, compared to control males. There was no correlation between increased body temperature and post-dose vomitus or emesis. No consistent dose-dependent or duration-dependent effects were observed. On day 25 of the recovery phase, a slight increase in body temperature was observed in males given 50 mg/kg neoGAA spiked with 3 or 6 mg/kg glycan (1-0.5°C) and in females given 50 mg/kg neoGAA spiked with 6 mg/kg glycan (0.7°C). Animals in the high-dose group had no increased body temperature. Since there was only one animal/sex in the
L1745: recovery group, it is difficult to establish conclusions from the recovery group data. Thus, the change in body temperature was not considered to be treatment-related.
L1746: ## Hematology
L1747: Blood samples for hematology and coagulation were collected from fasted animals via the femoral vein on days 26, 41, and 71 of the pre-dose phase, at approximately 24 hours after the end of infusion on days 30, 58, and 86 of the dosing phase, and on the days of scheduled necropsies (day 92 of the dosing phase and day 29 of the recovery phase).
L1748: There was a trend towards a slight but non-significant decrease (↓up to 6%) in red cell mass (RBC, hemoglobin, and Hct). No other changes were observed during the treatment period. The recovery group data is inconclusive due to the use of only one animal/sex in each group.
L1749: ## Clinical Chemistry
L1750: Blood samples for clinical chemistry were collected from fasted animals via the femoral vein on days 26, 41, and 71 of the pre-dose phase, at approximately 24 hours after the end of infusion on days 30, 58, and 86 of the dosing phase, and on the days of scheduled necropsies (day 92 of the dosing phase and day 29 of the recovery phase). There were no treatment-related effects.
L1751: ## Urinalysis
L1752: Urine samples were collected during the overnight period on days 41 and 44 of the pre-dose phase, on day 92 of the dosing phase, and on day 29 of the recovery phase. There were no treatment-related changes.
L1753: ## Gross Pathology
L1754: All animals were subjected to necropsy at termination. There were no treatment-related changes.
L1755: ## Organ Weights
L1756: At scheduled termination, the following organs (when present) were weighed (paired organs were weighed together): adrenal, brain, epididymis, heart, kidney, lung, liver, gall bladder, ovary, pituitary gland, prostate, seminal vesicle, spleen, testis, thymus, thyroid with parathyroid, and uterus.
L1757: There was a dose-dependent decrease in absolute and relative weights of epididymis (at all doses), prostate, and testis (mid- and high-dose levels) as shown in the table below. The recovery group data is inconclusive due to the use of only one animal/sex in each group.

L1761: In the 26-week IV toxicity study in monkeys submitted in the initial IND, neoGAA at 50 mg/kg every other week had no effects on absolute and relative weights of epididymis, testis, and prostate, whereas 200 mg/kg decreased absolute weights of epididymis (\downarrow23%) and testes (\downarrow16%). Thus, it is likely the decreased absolute and relative weights in male reproductive organs are related to the free glycan. However, no histopathological changes were observed in these organs.

L1769: ## Histological Findings
L1770: Testes were described as immature (4/4, 3/4, 4/4, and 3/4 in groups 1, 2, 3 and 4, respectively), or peri-pubertal (1/4 and 1/4 in the groups 2 and 4). The different stages of maturity in testis among the males is unlikely to be related to the decrease in absolute and relative weights of testis in the treatment groups.
L1771: Focal right atrial endocardial thickening was observed in each of the four females in all treatment groups, with a severity of slight to moderate (high-dose female only). In addition, as shown in the Sponsor's table below, intima or endocardium thickening at the infusion site A (anterior vena cava or right atrium of heart, 1 to 2 cm anterior to the catheter tip) was observed at comparable incidence and severity in all animals with or without treatment. Thus, it is likely that the focal right atrial endocardial thickening (hyperplasia) in females was procedure-related.

L1774: ## Special Evaluation
L1775: ## Anti-Drug Antibody Sample Collection
L1776: Blood samples were collected and stored for potential analysis of anti-drug antibody prior to treatment and on day 84.
L1777: ## Toxicokinetics
L1778: Blood samples were collected on days 1 and 85 of the dosing phase at the following time-points: within 5 minutes of the end of infusion and at approximately 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 24, and 48 hours after the end of infusion. The concentrations of neoGAA in serum were determined using a validated 4-methylumbelliferyl fluorescence assay. The lower limit of quantitation (LLOQ) was 0.1 µg/mL.
L1779: Following single and repeated administration of 50 mg/kg neoGAA spiked with glycan, neoGAA concentrations were quantifiable in all animals up to 54 hours from the start of infusion. Maximum concentrations were generally observed at the end of infusion. There was no accumulation following repeated dosing. Glycan had no effects on systemic exposure to neoGAA. There were no apparent sex-related differences, although females showed a slightly lower exposure (female to male AUC0-54hr ratios of
L1780: 0.640 to 0.938 on day 1 and 0.625 to 0.881 on day 85). The PK parameters are summarized in the Sponsor's table below.
L1781: ## Mean Serum Toxicokinetic Parameters of neoGAA

L1784: ## Dosing Solution Analysis
L1785: ## neoGAA:
L1786: Protein concentrations for vehicle and neoGAA dosing formulations prepared for administration on days 1, 15, 43, and 85 of the dosing phase were analyzed. Concentrations for neoGAA formulations for all dose groups were all within the acceptable range of \pm 20% of the expected concentration. Percent recovery values ranged from 86.4% (only formulation for group 1 on day 1) to 117.7% with %CV values \le 3.9%. The formulations were stable when compared to concentrations from pre-dose and post-dose samples.
L1787: ## Spiked Glycan:
L1788: Glycan concentrations in the formulation for administration on day 1 were 53.2-74.7% of expected concentrations due to lower concentration of the glycan stock solution (1.864 mg/mL rather 2.5 mg/mL). Therefore, the formulations for subsequent dosing on days 15, 43, and 85 were prepared using another glycan stock solution containing 2.37 mg/mL. As shown in the Sponsor's table below, glycan concentrations in the formulation for group 2 for administration on days 43 and 85 were 73.7-79.5% of expected concentration. However, the glycan concentrations in formulations for groups 3 and 4 for all dosing days ranged from 81.6-112.5% of the expected concentrations. The glycan concentrations in formulations for the group 4 animals were 93-112.5% of the expected concentration on dosing days 15, 43, and 85. Therefore, animals in group 4 (high-dose) were exposed to the intended concentration (dose) of glycan, in contrast to animals in groups 2 and 3. Thus, the lower than expected exposure to glycan in groups 2 and 3 is unlikely to impact the overall conclusions of the study.

L1791: ## **Summary and Safety Evaluation:**
L1792: In the submitted 13-week toxicity study, cynomolgus monkeys were treated with 50 mg/kg neoGAA spiked with 3, 6, or 12.55 mg/kg free glycan (unconjugated Genz-669342) every other week intravenously. Emesis and vomitus were observed in all treatment group animals following infusion, although this effect was not dose-dependent. All doses of the test article produced slight to moderate reductions in absolute and relative weights of epididymis, prostate, and testis. However, no microscopic changes were observed in these tissues. Therefore, the weight reductions in male reproductive organs are not considered adverse. No target organs of toxicity were identified. The NOAEL for free glycan was considered to be 12.55 mg/kg.
L1793: The weight reductions in male reproductive organs appear to be caused by the free glycan, based on the dose-dependency observed in the present study using spiked neoGAA, and the previous 26-week toxicity study in monkeys with multiple dose levels of neoGAA that contained a lower level of free glycan.
L1794: The Sponsor submitted the current amendment to support a proposed increase in the specification limit for residual glycan (unconjugated Genz-669342) in the drug product, based on the current 13-week toxicity study in monkeys. The Sponsor has increased the residual glycan specification from [REDACTED](b)(4) µg/mL in the drug product.
L1795: The establishment of a NOAEL in monkeys at 12.55 mg/kg free glycan (150.6 mg/m2) in the 13-week toxicity study results in a safety margin of 9.7 for the amended limit of [REDACTED](b)(4) µg/mL (based on [REDACTED](b)(4) mg/m2 residual glycan as the worst-case exposure with administration of 20 mg/kg neoGAA [REDACTED](b)(4) mL/kg in adult patients). The dose of 20 mg/kg neoGAA is the highest dose tested in clinical studies. Therefore, the submitted toxicity study in monkeys qualifies residual glycan at the amended limit of [REDACTED](b)(4) µg/mL in the drug product.
L1796: ## **RECOMMENDATIONS:**
L1797: From a nonclinical viewpoint, the proposed increase in the residual glycan specification to ≤ [REDACTED](b)(4) µg/mL in drug product is acceptable.

Pharmacologist, DGIEP

L1810: ## DEPARTMENT OF HEALTH AND HUMAN SERVICES
## PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICE
## FOOD AND DRUG ADMINISTRATION
## CENTER FOR DRUG EVALUATION AND RESEARCH
L1811: ## PHARMACOLOGY/TOXICOLOGY IND REVIEW AND EVALUATION
L1812: Application number: 109569
Supporting document/s: 135
Sponsor's letter date: April 3, 2018
CDER stamp date: April 3, 2018
Product: NeoGAA (Avalglucosidase)
Indication: Pompe disease
Sponsor: Sanofi Genzyme
Cambridge, MA
Review Division: Gastroenterology and Inborn Errors Products
Reviewer: Fang Cai, PhD
Supervisor/Team Leader: David B. Joseph, PhD
Acting Division Director: Roman Dragos, MD
Project Manager: Jenny Doan
L1813: ## 1. Background
L1814: Pompe disease (also known as acid maltase deficiency or glycogen storage disease (GSD) type II) is a rare, autosomal recessive genetic disease caused by the deficiency of lysosomal acid \alpha-glucosidase (GAA). Due to lack of GAA, accumulation of glycogen in body tissues, especially cardiac muscle and respiratory and limb skeletal muscle, results in changes in architecture and function of the affected myocytes and leads to multi-systemic pathology and ultimately death. The approved recombinant human acid \alpha-glucosidase (rhGAA, alglucosidase alfa with the trade names Myozyme® and Lumizyme®) improved cardiac muscle function, but not skeletal muscle. rhGAA is transferred to lysosomes via the cation-independent mannose-6-phosphate receptor (CIM6Pr), which thus plays an essential role in cellular binding and uptake. Furthermore, studies showed that the CIM6Pr is present in relatively low levels in human skeletal muscle compared to that of cardiac tissue, and that bis-phosphorylated glycans (bis-M6P) have a significantly higher affinity for the CIM6Pr as compared to the mono-phosphorylated forms. Therefore, it is plausible that increased levels of bis-M6P on rhGAA may enhance the uptake of rhGAA into skeletal muscle.
L1815: NeoGAA (Avalglucosidase) is a modified form of the approved drug alglucosidase alfa (Myozyme® and Lumizyme®), an enzyme replacement therapy (ERT). The active ingredient in the neoGAA drug product is a chemically-modified form of recombinant human \alpha-glucosidase (rhGAA) expressed in Chinese Hamster Ovary (CHO) cells. NeoGAA is produced by conjugating a synthetic glycan to oxidized sialic acid residues on alglucosidase alfa through a synthetic linker with an aminoxy moiety. NeoGAA binding to M6Pr on the cell surface is mediated by the conjugated synthetic bismannose-6-phosphate-Man6 glycan. After neoGAA is internalized and transported into lysosomes, neoGAA degrades glycogen into glucose by catalyzing the hydrolysis of \alpha-1,4- and \alpha-1,6- glycosidic linkages of lysosomal glycogen.
L1816: In GAA knock out mice, neoGAA at doses of 4, 12, and 20 mg/kg/week produced glycogen reduction in the heart, quadriceps, and diaphragm similar to that observed with rhGAA administered at 20, 60 and 100 mg/kg, respectively. The results suggest that neoGAA was approximately 5-fold more potent than rhGAA under the study conditions.
L1817: ## 2. Assessment of Carcinogenicity Potential
L1818: The Sponsor submitted an assessment of carcinogenicity potential for neoGAA, but did not conduct any carcinogenicity studies. The Sponsor proposed that carcinogenicity studies are not necessary based on their assessment.
L1819: NeoGAA is a chemically-modified lysosomal enzyme that degrades glycogen into glucose by catalyzing the hydrolysis of \alpha-1,4- and \alpha-1,6-glycosidic linkages of lysosomal glycogen. The mechanism of action does not raise a concern regarding the potential for neoplasm induction or tumor promotion.
L1820: However, neoGAA is produced by conjugating a synthetic glycan (Genz-669342) to oxidized sialic acid residues on rhGAA. Genz-669342 contains six mannose units and a synthetic linker with an aminoxy moiety. The structure of neoGAA is shown below, with the linker displayed as blue.
L1821: Figure 1 - Avalglucosidase alpha structural formula
L1822: Genz-669342 was negative in the Ames test and chromosomal aberration test in cultured human peripheral blood lymphocytes. In addition, neoGAA at up to 150 mg/kg was negative in two micronucleus assays in GAA knock out mice. Thus, neither neoGAA nor its synthetic glycan moiety exhibits genotoxic potential.
L1823: Monkeys that were administered neoGAA (50 mg/kg) spiked with Genz-669342 at doses up to 12.55 mg/kg IV every other week for 13 weeks did not show any proliferative lesions. Furthermore, there were no proliferative lesions in the 26-week monkey study, in which the animals were treated with neoGAA at doses up to 200 mg/kg IV every other week.
L1824: The carcinogenic potential of neoGAA appears to be low, based on the mechanism of action and the toxicity profile of the synthetic glycan moiety (Genz-669342). However, the available data does not provide an adequate basis for evaluating the carcinogenicity potential of the linker moiety of Genz-669342, and the Sponsor has not provided information about the metabolic fate of the linker moiety. Given the possibility that the linker moiety may be released in an unconjugated form after administration of neoGAA (i.e. cleaved from both GAA and the oligosaccharide moiety of Genz-669342), additional information is needed to support the Sponsor's conclusion that carcinogenicity studies are unnecessary. Therefore, it is premature to agree to a waiver of carcinogenicity studies.
L1825: To facilitate further evaluation of the carcinogenicity potential of neoGAA, the Sponsor should provide an estimate of the worst case exposure (on a mg/kg or \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} basis) to the unconjugated linker moiety of Genz-669342. This estimate should include the maximum amount of linker that could be released from unconjugated [REDACTED] (b) (4) present in the drug product at the acceptance criterion (i.e. [REDACTED] \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} Genz-
L1826: 669342 for the clinical dose of 20 mg/kg neoGAA), in addition to the maximum amount of linker that could be released from neoGAA. The Sponsor should provide all details of the supporting calculations for this estimate.
L1827: To address our concern about the potential release of the linker moiety after administration of neoGAA, we will also consider ADME data that directly describes the metabolic fate and excretion of the linker moiety, if any such data is available.
L1828: ## RECOMMENDATIONS:
L1829: - We do not agree with the Sponsor's conclusion that carcinogenicity studies are unnecessary. Since the metabolic fate of the synthetic linker moiety of Genz-669342 is unknown, there is a deficiency in the current dataset that precludes an adequate assessment of the carcinogenicity potential of neoGAA. The available data does not provide an adequate basis for evaluating the carcinogenicity potential of the unconjugated synthetic linker moiety, which may be released after administration of neoGAA.
- To facilitate further evaluation of the carcinogenicity potential of neoGAA, the Sponsor should provide an estimate of the worst case exposure (on a mg/kg or \mug/kg basis) to the unconjugated linker moiety of Genz-669342. This estimate should include the maximum amount of linker that could be released from unconjugated [REDACTED] (b) (4) present in the drug product at the acceptance criterion (i.e. [REDACTED] (b) (4) \mug/mL (b) (4) for the clinical dose of 20 mg/kg neoGAA), in addition to the maximum amount of linker that could be released from neoGAA (based on assumption of 100% release of the linker). The Sponsor should provide all details of the supporting calculations for this estimate.
- To address our concern about the potential release of the linker moiety after administration of neoGAA, the Sponsor can also submit ADME data that directly describes the metabolic fate and excretion of the linker moiety, if any such data is available. However, an estimate of the worst case exposure to the unconjugated linker moiety should still be provided, as requested above.

**Supporting document/s:** 18

**Sponsor's letter date:** December 4, 2013

**Product:** neoGAA (recombinant human \alpha-glucosidase conjugated with synthetic bis-mannose-6-phosphate-Man6 glycan)

**Indication:** Treatment of Pompe disease

**Sponsor:** Genzyme Corporation
Cambridge, MA

**Review Division:** Division of Gastroenterology and Inborn Errors Products

L1841: **Drug:** neoGAA; a recombinant human acid \alpha-glucosidase (rhGAA) expressed in Chinese Hamster Ovary (CHO) cells, conjugated to multiple copies of a synthetic bis-mannose-6-phosphate-containing hexasaccharide (Genz-669342).
L1842: **Molecular formula/molecular weight:** The approximate molecular formula is alglucosidase alfa (C_{4488}H_{6826}N_{1196}O_{1297}S_{33}, protein formula) + 6-8 glycan units (C_{42}H_{75}N_{3}O_{40}P_{2}). NeoGAA has an approximate total molecular weight of 120,000 Da.
L1843: **Pharmacological class:** enzyme replacement therapy
L1844: **Indication:** treatment of Pompe disease
L1845: **Route of administration:** intravenous
L1846: ## **Background:**
L1847: The Sponsor submitted this IND for the development of neoGAA for treatment of Pompe disease. The initial submission contained a protocol for a Phase 1, multicenter, multinational, open-label, ascending dose, intravenous infusion study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of neoGAA in patients with Pompe disease. Patients in each group will receive 13 bi-weekly intravenous infusions of neoGAA for a total of 24 weeks (week 1 to week 25). The dose levels will be 5, 10, and 20 mg/kg.
L1848: NeoGAA is produced by conjugating a synthetic glycan (Genz-669342) to oxidized sialic acid residues on rhGAA through a synthetic linker with an aminoxy moiety. The synthetic linker portion of Genz-669342 is structurally similar to hydroxylamine, which has the potential to be genotoxic. However, Genz-669342 was negative in the Ames test and chromosomal aberration assay. In the initial IND submitted on 3/28/2013, the Sponsor proposed a specification limit of \mu\text{g}/\text{ml} for unconjugated Genz-669342 in the drug product. At this limit, the maximum potential daily dose of unconjugated Genz-669342 from neoGAA would have been \text{mg} (assuming a 60-kg body weight, and a dose of 20 mg/kg neoGAA), which greatly exceeded the recommended ICH limit (i.e. \text{mg}/\text{day} for a 60-kg patient). Although the neoGAA batch used in the toxicology studies contained unconjugated Genz-669342, the pivotal 26-week IV toxicity study in monkeys did not provide an assurance of safety for the proposed specification limit of \mu\text{g}/\text{ml}.
L1849: A teleconference was held with the Sponsor on April 25, 2013 to discuss the safety concern related to the proposed limit for unconjugated Genz-669342 (free glycan). The Sponsor agreed to the Agency's recommended limit of \mu\text{g}/\text{ml}, and submitted a written response on April 26, 2013 (SD # 5) that reflects this agreement. The following recommendation was communicated in a letter to the Sponsor dated July 26, 2013:
L1850: **"In the absence of additional nonclinical data, the limit for free glycan (i.e., unconjugated Genz-669342) in the drug product should not exceed the current limit of \mu\text{g}/\text{ml}. If you plan to increase the limit for free glycan, submit a**
L1851: proposal for a chronic toxicity study to support the safety of a higher limit, in addition to the proposed new limit for free glycan."
L1852: The details of the safety assessment of unconjugated Genz-669342 and rationale for the recommended limit in the drug product are described in the Pharmacology/ Toxicology preliminary safety review dated July 3, 2013. On August 23, 2013, the Sponsor submitted a protocol outline for a 3-month toxicity study in monkeys to support a higher limit for free glycan levels in the drug product. During the review process, an information request was sent to the Sponsor to clarify the dose volume in the proposed study and the intended new specification limit for free glycan. The Sponsor submitted a revised study protocol on December 4, 2013, and also indicated their intent to increase the limit for free glycan in the drug product to (b)(4) \mug/ml. The proposed study is reviewed below.
L1853: ## Study Outline for a 3-Month Repeated Dose Toxicity Study of neoGAA with Elevated Residual Glycan Levels in Cynomolgous Monkeys
L1854: ## Sponsor's proposed study design:
L1855: The proposed study will be GLP compliant. Cynomolgous monkeys at least 18 months of age will be treated intravenously with neoGAA containing different levels of residual glycan (i.e. unconjugated Genz-669342). neoGAA will be administered as a 6-hr infusion once every two weeks for 13 weeks (total of 7 doses). The study design is shown in the Sponsor's table below. The dose volume will be approximately 20 ml/kg, with all groups receiving 50 mg/kg neoGAA. The vehicle will be a solution containing 10 mM histidine pH 6 (b)(4) 0.01% Tween 80, 2% mannitol, and 2% glycine. The recovery groups will be sacrificed at 4 weeks after the final dose. Clinical signs, mortality, food consumption, body weight, respiratory rate, heart rate, core temperature, and blood pressure will be monitored. Evaluations of hematology, clinical chemistry, urinalysis, and ophthalmology will be performed. Blood samples for toxicokinetics evaluation will be collected at the following time-points after the 1st and 7th doses: pre-dose (within 24 hours), immediately at the end of infusion, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 24, and 48 hours after the end of infusion. Blood samples for measurement of anti-neoGAA antibodies will be collected prior to the first and seventh doses, but will only be analyzed if necessary. All animals will be subjected to necropsy and histopathological examination. (b)(4)

L1858: ## Comments:
L1859: - The Agency requested a chronic toxicity study (i.e. 6 months in rodents or 9 months in nonrodents for small molecules) to support the qualification of free glycan (unconjugated Genz-669342) at levels greater than 50 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} in the drug product. However, the Sponsor has proposed a 3-month toxicity study in cynomolgus monkeys. Since the duration of the proposed study is in accordance with recommendations in ICH guidances Q3A and Q3B, the proposed study duration is acceptable.
- The proposed dose level of neoGAA (50 mg/kg) in all treatment groups is acceptable, given that this dose was the NOAEL in the 26-week toxicity study in monkeys (see Pharmacology/Toxicology preliminary safety review dated July 3, 2013).
- The establishment of a NOAEL or a tolerated dose in monkeys at the 600 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} free glycan level (i.e. 144 \text{mg}/\text{m}^2) in the proposed study will result in a safety margin of (b) (4) for the proposed limit of (b) (4) \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} (i.e. (b) (4) \text{mg}/\text{m}^2 in humans), based on the intended dose volumes of 20 \text{mL}/\text{kg} in monkeys and (b) (4) \text{mL}/\text{kg} in humans. Therefore, the proposed study can qualify free glycan at the proposed limit of (b) (4) \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} in the drug product. However, toxicity that may occur in monkeys at the higher levels of free glycan ((b) (4) \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}) should also be considered in the safety assessment of the proposed limit.
- The study should include 4 monkeys/sex in the groups designated for sacrifice at the end of the treatment period.
- The proposed control group (b) (4) is unacceptable. A vehicle control group should be included in this study.
L1860: ## **RECOMMENDATIONS:**
L1861: 1. The proposed study should include 4 monkeys/sex in the groups designated for sacrifice at the end of the treatment period.
L1862: 2. The proposed control group [REDACTED] (b) (4) is unacceptable. A vehicle control group should be included in this study.

L1891: ## Introduction and Proposed Clinical Study:
L1892: NeoGAA is a modified form of the approved drug alglucosidase alfa (Myozyme® and Lumizyme®), an enzyme replacement therapy (ERT). The active ingredient in the neoGAA drug product (lyophilized powder for solution) is recombinant human \alpha-glucosidase (rhGAA) expressed in Chinese Hamster Ovary (CHO) cells, conjugated with synthetic bismannose-6-phosphate-Man6 glycan. GAA is a lysosomal enzyme that degrades glycogen into glucose by catalyzing the hydrolysis of \alpha-1,4- and \alpha-1,6- glycosidic linkages of lysosomal glycogen. Pompe disease (also known as acid maltase deficiency or glycogen storage disease (GSD) type II) is a rare, autosomal recessive genetic disease caused by the deficiency of lysosomal acid \alpha-glucosidase (GAA). The resulting accumulation of glycogen in body tissues, especially cardiac muscle and respiratory and limb skeletal muscle, disrupts the architecture and function of the affected myocytes leading to multi-systemic pathology and ultimately death. The approved recombinant human acid \alpha-glucosidase (rhGAA, alglucosidase alfa with the trade names Myozyme® and Lumizyme®) efficiently improved cardiac muscle function, but not skeletal muscle. rhGAA is transferred to lysosomes via the cation-independent mannose-6-phosphate receptor (CIM6Pr), which thus plays an essential role in cellular binding and uptake. Furthermore, studies showed that the CIM6Pr is present in relatively low levels in human skeletal muscle compared to that of cardiac tissue, and that bis-phosphorylated glycans (bis-M6P) have a significantly higher affinity for the CIM6Pr as compared to the mono-phosphorylated forms. Therefore, it is plausible that increased levels of bis-M6P on rhGAA may enhance the uptake of rhGAA into skeletal muscle. Thus, the Sponsor is developing recombinant human \alpha-glucosidase (rhGAA) conjugated with synthetic bismannose-6-phosphate-Man6 glycan as a second generation therapy for Pompe disease.
L1893: In the present IND, the Sponsor proposes a Phase 1, multicenter, multinational, open-label, ascending dose, intravenous infusion study to evaluate the efficacy
L1894: and safety of neoGAA in patients with Pompe disease. A total of 21 patients at least 18 years of age will be enrolled in this study. Naïve patients with late-onset Pompe disease will be assigned to group 1 (n = 9) and late-onset Pompe disease patients previously treated with alglucosidase alfa will be assigned to group 2 (n = 12). Patients in each group will receive 13 bi-weekly intravenous infusions of neoGAA for a total of 24 weeks (week 1 to week 25). Group 1 and group 2 will be initiated simultaneously; each group will have 3 dose levels. Subjects are not allowed to participate in more than 1 dose group in the study. The dose levels will be 5, 10, or 20 mg/kg. Three patients will be included in each dose cohort in group 1. In group 2, each of the 5 and 10 mg/kg dose cohorts will include 3 patients, and 6 patients will be in the 20 mg/kg cohort. Dosing in the next cohort will begin after safety assessment is completed following 3 infusions in the previous cohort. Patients will receive each dose level bi-weekly for a total of 13 doses, and the dose volume will be determined by bodyweight and dose level. The study design is summarized in the table below.
L1895: **Table 2: Study Design**

L1897: Vital signs, ECGs, safety laboratory analysis (liver function test, renal function test, clinical chemistry, urinalysis, and hematology) and adverse events will be monitored pre-dose, during the treatment period, and at week 25. Physical examinations will be performed. Immunogenicity (anti-neoGAA IgG and neutralizing antibodies in IgG positive patients, and anti-alglucosidase alfa IgG antibodies) will be evaluated. Blood samples will be collected at weeks 1, 13, and 25 to analyze neoGAA levels. Pharmacodynamic markers (e.g. skeletal muscle biopsy and urine Hex4) will be monitored pre-dose, during the treatment period, and at weeks 25 and 27. Function tests including the 6 minute walk test, GSGC (Gait, Stair, Gower's Maneuver, and Chair), Gross Motor Function Measure-88, Quick Motor Function Test, and pulmonary function testing, with additional patient assessment through the PedsQL (Pediatric Quality of Life Inventory Multidimensional Fatigue Scale) will be conducted pre-dose and at weeks 13 and 25.
L1898: To support the proposed clinical study, the Sponsor submitted the following nonclinical studies: pharmacology studies, pharmacokinetics (plasma kinetics and distribution), repeated-dose intravenous toxicity studies in mice and monkeys, and genotoxicity studies on a glycan(b) (4). The safety
L1899: pharmacology of neoGAA was assessed in a 26-week toxicity study in cynomolgus monkeys. No reproductive and developmental toxicity studies were submitted.
L1900: ## Pharmacology Studies
L1901: ## Glycogen Clearance Following Administration of 4 Weekly Doses of neoGAA and rhGAA in GAA Knockout Mice (Genzyme Study 07-1948)
L1902: NeoGAA (lot #: 07-BD-0418) was administered to GAA knockout mice (3/sex/group, 3 - 6 months old) via intravenous injection at doses of 4, 12, and 20 mg/kg/week for 4 weeks. For comparison, GAA mice (3/sex/group, 3 - 6 months old) were treated with rhGAA at doses of 20, 60, and 100 mg/kg/week via IV injection for 4 weeks. Starting with the second dose, mice were pretreated with diphenhydramine (5 mg/kg IP) at 10-20 minutes prior to dosing with the test article, to prevent hypersensitivity reactions. All animals were sacrificed 7 days following the last dose. Glycogen depletion in heart, quadriceps, and triceps was assessed using a biochemical glycogen assay. Serum was analyzed for anti-drug antibodies.
L1903: There was a dose-dependent reduction of glycogen in heart, quadriceps, and triceps in neoGAA- and rhGAA-treated animals. After 4 weekly treatments, administration of 12 and 20 mg/kg neoGAA resulted in equivalent glycogen depletion in heart compared to groups administered 60 and 100 mg/kg rhGAA, respectively. NeoGAA at 4 mg/kg significantly increased glycogen depletion in the heart tissue compared to 20 mg/kg rhGAA. Similar glycogen depletion was observed in the skeletal muscle of treated animals following administration of 4, 12 and 20 mg/kg neoGAA compared to 20, 60 and 100 mg/kg rhGAA, respectively. The results suggest that neoGAA was approximately 5-fold more potent than rhGAA under the study conditions.
L1904: Anti-drug antibodies were identified in all treated animals with no differences between dose levels and across treatments. There appeared to be a dose responsive increase in antibody titers in animals treated with neoGAA.
L1905: ## Glycogen Clearance Following Administration of 4 Weekly Doses of rhGAA and neoGAA in GAA Knockout Mice (Genzyme Study 10-00587)
L1906: NeoGAA (lot #: NE006, also used in the 26-week toxicology study in cynomolgus monkeys) was administered to GAA knockout mice (6/sex/group, 3 - 6 months old) via intravenous injection at doses of 4, 12, and 20 mg/kg/week for 4 weeks. For comparison, GAA knockout mice (3/sex/group, 3 - 6 months old) were treated with rhGAA at doses of 20, 60, and 100 mg/kg/week via IV injection for 4 weeks. Starting with the second dose, mice were pretreated with diphenhydramine (5 mg/kg IP) at 10-20 minutes prior to dosing to prevent hypersensitivity reactions. All animals were sacrificed 7 days following the last dose. Glycogen depletion in heart, quadriceps, triceps, psoas, and diaphragm
L1907: was evaluated using a biochemical glycogen assay. Serum was analyzed for anti-drug antibodies.
L1908: NeoGAA at doses of 4, 12, and 20 mg/kg/week produced a similar glycogen reduction in the heart, quadriceps and diaphragm tissue compared to rhGAA administered at 20, 60 and 100 mg/kg, respectively. Glycogen levels were similar in the triceps muscle following administration of 4 and 12 mg/kg neoGAA compared to 20 and 60 mg/kg rhGAA, respectively. However, glycogen levels in triceps muscle were significantly lower following administration of 100 mg/kg rhGAA compared to 20 mg/kg neoGAA. Glycogen levels in the psoas of mice dosed with 12 mg/kg neoGAA were similar to mice that received 60 mg/kg rhGAA. However, 20 mg/kg neoGAA did not produce a similar reduction of glycogen in psoas as that in mice treated with rhGAA at 100 mg/kg. NeoGAA at 4 mg/kg and rhGAA at 20 mg/kg had no effects on glycogen levels in psoas. Overall, the results indicated that neoGAA and rhGAA reduced glycogen to similar levels in most tissues, and that neoGAA was approximately 5-fold more potent than rhGAA. Antibody titers were similar between all groups of animals irrespective of test article or dose level administered.
L1909: ## Pharmacodynamics of neoGAA Produced from rhGAA Containing Different Levels of Sialic Acid following 4 Weekly Intravenous Doses in the GAA Knockout Mouse (Genzyme Study 11-02367)
L1910: The objective of this study was to evaluate glycogen clearance from tissues in the GAA knockout mouse following administration of neoGAA produced from rhGAA containing either 5.5 mol/mol sialic acid (neoGAA-5SA) or 7.2 mol/mol sialic acid (neoGAA-7SA). These two lots of neoGAA contained approximately 5 or 7 synthetic glycan moieties (Genz-669342) per neoGAA molecule, respectively.
L1911: GAA knockout mice (3/sex/group, 3 - 6 months old) were administered either neoGAA-5SA or neoGAA-7SA via intravenous injection at doses of 0, 4, and 12 mg/kg/week for 4 weeks. Starting with the second dose, mice were pre-treated with diphenhydramine at 5 mg/kg IP 10-20 minutes prior to dosing with the test article to prevent hypersensitivity reactions. All animals were sacrificed 7 days following the last dose. Glycogen depletion in heart, quadriceps, and triceps was evaluated using a biochemical glycogen assay.
L1912: NeoGAA-5SA and neoGAA-7SA at doses of 4 and 12 mg/kg/week produced similar glycogen depletion in the heart. NeoGAA-5SA and neoGAA-7SA at doses of 4 and 12 mg/kg had little or no effects on glycogen level in quadriceps. In triceps, glycogen was depleted with administration of 12 mg/kg neoGAA-7SA, whereas no depletion was observed with neoGAA-5SA. Under the study conditions, the results suggested that there was no consistent difference in tissue glycogen reduction following administration of neoGAA when produced from rhGAA containing 5.5 or 7.2 mol/mol sialic acid.
L1913: ## Safety Pharmacology
L1914: The effects of neoGAA in the CNS, pulmonary system, and cardiovascular system were evaluated in the 26-week intravenous toxicity study in cynomolgus monkeys (see Toxicology section below). Hemodynamic data (systolic, diastolic, and mean arterial pressure) was not monitored in this study. The Sponsor will monitor hemodynamics closely in the clinical trial.
L1915: ## Toxicology
L1916: ## 1. Repeated-dose Toxicity
L1917: ## Intravenous Administration 50 mg/kg neoGAA or rhGAA Every Other Day for 14 Days (b) (4) Study FFA00125)
L1918: This was not a GLP study. CD-1 mice (10/sex/group, 8-10 weeks old) were treated with vehicle, neoGAA, or rhGAA at doses of 0 or 50 mg/kg via IV bolus injection to the tail vein on days 1, 3, 5, 7, 9, 11, and 13. Starting on day 9 (dose number 5), animals were administered 5 mg/kg diphenhydramine (DPH) intraperitoneally (IP) at 10-20 minutes prior to test article administration to prevent hypersensitivity reactions; the DPH pretreatment was continued for all doses thereafter. Daily clinical observations and weekly body weight and food consumption were recorded. On day 14, all animals were euthanized and subjected to a complete gross necropsy and tissue collection. The following organs were weighed: (adrenal glands, brain, heart, kidney, lung, liver, spleen, pancreas, pituitary gland, epididymis, prostate, salivary glands, seminal vesicles, testes, thymus, thyroid gland, quadriceps, and triceps). Selected tissues were examined microscopically (brain, heart, kidney, lung, liver, spleen, pancreas, quadriceps, triceps, and urinary bladder). The study design is described in the following table (taken from the study report).
L1919: **Table 3: Study Design for a 14-day Toxicity Study in Mice**

L1922: There were no neoGAA or rhGAA treatment-related deaths. Administration of neoGAA or rhGAA had no effects on clinical signs, body weight, food consumption, hematology or clinical chemistry parameters. Spleen weights were increased in male animals treated with rhGAA (\uparrow 28\%, p \le 0.05) and neoGAA (\uparrow 41\%, p \le 0.05) and in female animals treated with neoGAA (\uparrow 32\%, p \le 0.05) when compared to controls. However, there were no associated histopathologic changes. Macroscopic findings including small testis, black and red foci over testis, and discoloration of testis were observed in 1 of 10 male animals from the rhGAA-treated group and 3 of 10 male animals from the neoGAA-treated groups. These tissues were not subjected to microscopic evaluation, and a relationship with administration of neoGAA could not be established. Male animals administered 50 mg/kg neoGAA had minimal multifocal hepatocellular necrosis throughout the parenchyma in 3 of 10 males, and minimal (2/10 males) to mild (1/10 males) inflammatory infiltrates in the hepatic parenchyma.
L1923: In conclusion, IV administration of rhGAA at 50 mg/kg every other day was tolerated in mice. rhGAA and neoGAA at 50 mg/kg were associated with macroscopic findings in the male reproductive tract (small testis, black and red foci over testis, discoloration of testis). In the liver, minimal to mild multifocal necrosis and inflammatory infiltrates were present after IV administration of neoGAA at 50 mg/kg every other day.
L1924: ## **28-Day Pilot Study of neoGAA Administered Intravenously Every Week to CD-1 Mice (Genzyme Study 10-00183)**
L1925: This was not a GLP study. Mice in the toxicokinetic (TK) groups (3/sex/group) received the same treatment regimen, and blood samples were collected at 5, 15, and 30 minutes, and 1, 2, 4, and 6 hours following the first and 4th dose. The study design is summarized in the Sponsor's table below.

L1929: Beginning with the third dose, animals in the 4 mg/kg/week group were treated with 10 mg/kg DPH (IP) at 10-20 minutes prior to dosing with the test article due to signs of hypersensitivity (e.g. lethargy and scruffy coat). Animals in the 40 and 120 mg/kg/week groups also were prophylactically treated with 10 mg/kg DPH IP, 10-20 minutes prior to administration of neoGAA. Thereafter, all mice were treated with DPH at a dose of 20 mg/kg IP, 10-20 minutes prior to administration of neoGAA. In some instances a second dose of DPH IP at 10-20 mg/kg was administered to animals that continued to show signs of hypersensitivity following neoGAA administration, despite pretreatment with DPH.
L1930: Clinical signs were observed prior to and following administration of each dose. Body weight was recorded weekly. On day 29, all animals were euthanized and subjected to a complete gross necropsy and tissue collection. Blood was collected for analysis of clinical pathology parameters and antibody levels. Organs (brain, heart, kidney, lung, liver, spleen, testes, and ovaries) were weighed. Tissues from all main study animals in groups 1 and 4 were evaluated through microscopic examination.
L1931: There were a total of 3 unscheduled deaths due to hypersensitivity reactions to neoGAA. Within 1 hr following administration of neoGAA on day 22, two males in the 4 mg/kg group died and one female in the 4 mg/kg group was euthanized. Hypersensitivity reactions (hunched position and lethargy) were observed in all neoGAA treatment groups beginning with the third dose. There were no treatment-related effects on body weight or body weight gain.
L1932: Compared to the vehicle control (group 1), animals in groups 3 and 4 had a significant decrease in triglycerides (213, 115, and 126 mg/dl for groups 1, 3 and 4, respectively, p &lt; 0.01). There was a dose-dependent increase in WBC (7.81 \times 10^3/\mu\text{l}, 7.36 \times 10^3/\mu\text{l}, 9.29 \times 10^3/\mu\text{l}, and 11^* \times 10^3/\mu\text{l} for groups 1, 2, 3 and 4, respectively, * p &lt; 0.01 compared to group 1 value). NeoGAA at 40 and 120 mg/kg produced an increase in absolute lymphocytes (15% and 45%, respectively), and neoGAA at all doses produced an increase in relative lymphocyte count (up to 23%). The Sponsor stated that the reduction in triglycerides in neoGAA-treated animals was likely due to some variability in food consumption prior to blood collection. The increase in lymphocytes was attributed to the hypersensitivity response to neoGAA.
L1933: NeoGAA at all doses had no effects on organ weights, organ to body weight ratios, and organ to brain weight ratios. There were no treatment-related macroscopic or microscopic changes.
L1934: All mice treated with neoGAA were positive for anti-neoGAA antibodies (IgG).
L1935: Toxicokinetic parameters were available from only one animal following the fourth administration at 4 mg/kg due to unscheduled deaths. The increase in AUC with dose was disproportionately high. In addition, a dose-dependent increase in t_{1/2} and a dose-dependent decrease in clearance were observed. These results suggest that the major plasma clearance mechanism, presumably uptake in tissues by CIM6Pr, was saturated at the tested dose levels. There was no gender-related difference. TK data is summarized in the Sponsor's table below.

L1942: In summary, CD-1 mice were treated with neoGAA at doses of 0, 4, 40, or 120 mg/kg/week IV for 28-days. There were no treatment-related changes in body weight, body weight gain, hematology, or clinical chemistry. No macroscopic or microscopic changes were observed. Hypersensitivity reactions were observed in all treatment groups, and all mice treated with neoGAA were positive for anti-drug antibodies. Plasma kinetics were indicative of saturation of plasma clearance. The no-observed-adverse-effect-level (NOAEL) was 120 mg/kg/week (IV) in CD-1 mice, based on exclusion of the hypersensitivity reactions from the toxicity assessment.

L1944: Cynomolgus monkeys (2/sex/group) were administered vehicle or neoGAA at dose levels of 4, 40, and 120 mg/kg/dose via 6 hour IV infusion through a surgically implanted femoral catheter and vascular access port (VAP), once weekly for 28 days. The study design is summarized in the Sponsor's table below. Blood samples for TK analysis were collected immediately at the end of infusion following the first and last infusion.

L1948: Clinical signs, morbidity, and mortality were observed twice daily. Body weight was recorded prior to study and weekly thereafter. Blood samples for clinical pathology (hematology and clinical chemistry parameters) evaluations were collected from all animals pre-dose and prior to the terminal necropsy. Serum antibodies from all animals were determined on days -1 and 21. At termination, 7 days following the fourth dose, urine samples were collected and all animals were subjected to necropsy examinations. Organ weights (adrenal, brain, epididymis, heart, kidney, lung, liver, pituitary, salivary gland, spleen,
L1949: thyroid/parathyroid, thymus, testes, and ovaries) were recorded, and all tissues were subjected to microscopic examination.
L1950: There were no treatment-related changes in clinical signs, body weight, body weight gain, hematology, clinical chemistry, urinalysis, and coagulation parameters. Thyroid/parathyroid gland weights were higher in females at 120 mg/kg/dose compared to controls (\uparrow 82\%, \uparrow 80\%, and \uparrow 80\% for absolute weight, organ/body weight ratio, and organ/brain weight ratio, respectively). However, these increased weights were not considered treatment-related due to absence of similar finding in males and the lack of microscopic correlates. Minimal to moderate vascular degeneration/regeneration and minimal mononuclear cell infiltrate at the infusion site were observed in control animals and all animals treated with neoGAA. There were no treatment-related macroscopic or microscopic changes.
L1951: Due to the limited serum neoGAA concentration data available from the 4 mg/kg dose level, there was no TK data for this dose level. NeoGAA toxicokinetic parameters were evaluated following the first and fourth infusion of 40 and 120 mg/kg. Systemic exposure increased in a greater than dose proportional manner, particularly after the fourth infusion. AUC and C_{max} values from the 4th infusion were increased relative to those from the first infusion. These time-dependent changes were associated with a statistically significant decrease in clearance in the 120 mg/kg group. The change in TK parameters after repeated dosing may have been related to the presence of anti-drug antibodies. Half-life was increased and clearance was decreased with increased dose level. These dose-related changes in TK parameters were only a trend at the first infusion, but were statistically significant at the fourth infusion. The dose-dependent increase in t_{1/2} suggests that the major plasma clearance mechanism, presumably uptake in tissues by CIM6Pr, was saturated at the tested dose levels of neoGAA in cynomolgus monkeys. Overall, there was no gender-related difference in TK parameters. The TK data is summarized in the Sponsor's table below.

L1957: ## A 26-Week Intravenous Infusion Toxicity Study of neoGAA in Cynomolgus Monkeys (Study 0658-11097)
L1958: This was a GLP study. Cynomolgus monkeys (5-6/sex/group, at least 18 months of age) were administered vehicle or neoGAA at dose levels of 0 (vehicle), 50, and 200 mg/kg/dose via 6-hr IV infusion through a surgically implanted femoral catheter and vascular access port (VAP), every other week for 26 weeks followed by a 4-week recovery period. The vehicle was 10 mM L-histine, 2% glycine, 2% mannitol, and 0.01% polysorbate 80, pH 6.2. The study design is summarized in the Sponsor's table below. Due to an apparently compromised venous access port, one of the 200 mg/kg females was replaced on Day 8. This replacement monkey died prior to dosing on Day 13; therefore, a control female was moved to the 200 mg/kg dose group on day 13 resulting in 5 females in the vehicle control group.

L1961: Animal DEW3F32 was removed alive from study on Day 8 and replaced with animal DEW3F37. DEW3F37 was sacrificed moribund on Day 13 and animal DEW1F24 was moved to the high-dose group.
L1962: Mortality and clinical signs were observed twice daily. Body weight and food consumption were recorded weekly or daily, respectively. Electrocardiogram, neurobehavioral examination, ophthalmic examination, respiratory rate, heart rate, and core body temperature were performed or recorded pre-study, and during the treatment and recovery periods. Blood samples were collected prior to study, during the dosing period, and at the end of the treatment and recovery periods for evaluation of hematology, clinical chemistry, anti-neoGAA antibody titers, and TK parameters. Urine was collected from all animals at the scheduled euthanasia. All animals were subjected to necropsy and gross examination. Selected organs were weighed and a complete set of tissues from all dose groups were microscopically examined.
L1963: There were two unscheduled sacrifices. On day 13, one female monkey in group 3 (200 mg/kg/dose, DEW3F37) was sacrificed in a moribund condition prior to dosing. The Sponsor attributed the death to severe stress based on profound lymphoid depletion of the thymus, spleen, lymph nodes, and focal necrosis in the liver. On day 168, one female monkey in group 2 (50 mg/kg/dose, DEW2F29) was sacrificed in a moribund condition with lower body paralysis due to systemic inflammation resulting from contamination of the venous access port. This female had paralysis of the hind limbs and tail. Microscopic examination revealed suppurative inflammation of the meninges surrounding the lumbar spinal cord and hemorrhage into the submeningeal and interstitial areas of the spinal cord and cauda equina. Axonal swelling was noted throughout the nerve tracts comprising the cauda equina. There was marked inflammation and necrosis around the access port. Acute hemorrhage throughout the lobes of the lung and distension of the urinary bladder were observed.
L1964: There were no test article-related changes in CNS evaluation (activity level, fasciculations, movement of facial muscles, and visual field), respiratory rate, core temperatures, ophthalmic examination and ECG (heart rate, RR, QT, and QTc intervals). Animals in the 200 mg/kg group showed up to approximately 9%
L1965: reductions in body weight, body weight gain, and food consumption during the treatment period. Reduced food consumption was more evident in males. Whether the changes in body weight, body weight gain, and food consumption were reversible is unclear since the recovery groups included only one female control and two males or females in the 200 mg/kg/dose group. NeoGAA had no effects on clinical signs, hematology, coagulation, and clinical chemistry. Due to the low number of samples (n = 0 - 2) collected at necropsy by cystocentesis, the urinalysis data are not interpretable.
L1966: Monkeys in group 3 (200 mg/kg/dose) had slight reductions in the absolute weight of several organs (see table below), and this effect was dose-dependent in most of these organs. A similar magnitude of reductions in organ weight/body weight ratio and organ weight/brain weight ratio were observed in these organs. At the end of the recovery period, organ weights of epididymes and testes at 200 mg/kg were still lower than the control values (29% and 35%, respectively). In contrast, thymus weight increased by 23% in males in group 3 compared to the control group. No conclusion can be made for female organ weight changes in groups 2 and 3 since there was only one sample available in groups 1 and 2. There were no histopathologic changes in these organs.
